Moreover, if the first diagram commutes upto homotopy, then so does the second one.

2.

The maps discussed in
Definition

5.3
(ii) are functorial in the following sense:
if a commutative diagram in
\mathfrak{C}^{*}\times\mathfrak{H}
is given, as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(C^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\kappa,\iota)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\\
,B\rightarrow B^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\kappa^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\rightarrow C^{\prime}\\
,\mathcal{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}}

then the following diagram of spaces commute strictly:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(C,\mathcal{H}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}B^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(C^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\\
,B\rightarrow B^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\kappa,\iota)_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\kappa^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{C\rightarrow C^{\prime}\\
,\mathcal{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\}_{*}}

Moreover, if the first diagram commutes upto homotopy, then so does the second one.

Proof.

We only discuss the second assertion; the first one is similar (and easier).

Given
\Phi\in\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{0})
, the two routes in
Item

2
yield the following diagram at the algebra level:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}A\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}A^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}B^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\rightarrow A^{\prime},B\rightarrow B^{\prime},\mathcal{H_{0}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H_{0}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}A\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}A^{\prime}\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(\iota^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(\iota)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\rightarrow C^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.8392,0.3608,0.3608}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{A\rightarrow A^{\prime},B\rightarrow B^{\prime},\mathcal{H_{0}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H_{0}^{\prime}}\}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.8392,0.3608,0.3608}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{C\rightarrow C^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\}_{*}}

In the case of strict commutativity, we have to show that the outer part of the above diagram commutes strictly. We can see this by using the filler morphisms given in orange, and noting that the top and bottom rectangles commute strictly because of the functoriality of the flip isomorphisms, and the middle one does by our hypothesis.

Similarly, in the case of homotopy commutativity, the top and bottom rectangles still commute strictly, but the middle one does only upto homotopy by our hypothesis.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.

1.

Sums are natural with respect to maps mentioned in
Definition

5.3
(i), in the sense that the following diagram commutes strictly, where
\varphi:\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}A\rightarrow B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})
is a graded
*
-homomorphism, and
\iota_{0}:\mathcal{H}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}
,
\iota_{1}:\mathcal{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}
are even isometries:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}_{0})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(B,(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(B,\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(B,\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(B,\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi,\iota_{0})_{*}\times(\varphi,\iota_{1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi,\iota_{0}\oplus\iota_{1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}}

2.

Sums are natural with respect to maps mentioned in
Definition

5.3
(ii), in the sense that the following diagram commutes strictly, where
\kappa:A\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B\rightarrow C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})
is a graded
*
-homomorphism, and
\iota_{0}:\mathcal{H}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}
,
\iota_{1}:\mathcal{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}
are even isometries:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{0})\times\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(C,(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(C,\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(C,\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(C,\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sum}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\kappa,\iota_{0}\oplus\iota_{1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\kappa,\iota_{0})_{*}\times(\kappa,\iota_{1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sum}}

Proof.

We only talk about the second diagram; the first one is similar (and easier):
Given
\varphi_{0}\in\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{0}),\varphi_{1}\in\mathbb{K}(A\hat{\otimes}B,\mathcal{H}_{1})
, we have to check the commutativity of the following diagram at the algebra level:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\oplus\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}(\mathcal{S}\oplus\mathcal{S})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}A\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0})\oplus\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}A\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}(A\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0})\oplus A\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0})\oplus A\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}(A\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})\oplus C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{S}\hat{\otimes}B\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\oplus C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{K}((\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{0})\oplus(1\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{flip}A\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{S})\oplus(\operatorname{flip}A\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(\iota_{0})\oplus\kappa\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(\iota_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{flip}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}))\oplus(\operatorname{flip}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}(\varphi_{0}\oplus\varphi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{add}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}A\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(\iota_{0}\oplus\iota_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime})\penalty\ \&\penalty\ \mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\Psi}

It does commute, as can be seen by taking homogeneous elementary tensors.
∎

5.2
H-space structures

In the remainder of this section, we restrict our attention to graded Hilbert spaces whose
even and odd parts are countably infinite dimensional
. Under this condition, the spectral
K
-theory spaces underlying
\mathcal{H}
can be given a H-space structure.

For the convenience of the reader, we include the definition of
H
-space here:

Definition 5.6
.

A pointed topological space
(X,e)
is said to be an H-space if there is a continuous map
\mu:X\times X\rightarrow X
such that the two maps
x\mapsto\mu(x,e)
and
x\mapsto\mu(e,x)
are based homotopic to the identity map
X\rightarrow X
.

More details about H-spaces and their properties can be found in
[
12
]
,
[
6
]
.

For the remainder of this section, fix a graded
C^{*}
-algebra
A
, and a graded Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
whose even and odd parts countably infinite dimensional.

Definition 5.7
.

Let
u:\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
be an even unitary
7
7

7

it exists as
\mathcal{H}
is taken to be infinite dimensional.
.
Define a map

\mu_{u}:\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})

on
\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})
as the composite

\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\xrightarrow{s}\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})\xrightarrow{1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u)}\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})

when
s
is as in
Definition

5.1
.

Before proceeding further, we recall the “Dixmier-Douady swindle” (see
[
7
]
for a proof):

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{1}
,
\mathcal{K}_{2}
be (ungraded) Hilbert spaces with
\mathcal{K}_{2}
countably infinite dimensional. Then,
\operatorname{Iso}(\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2})
and
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2})
(when
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
are unitarily isomorphic) are contractible in the strong
*
-topology.

In what follows, fix an even unitary
u:\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, whereby getting a map
\mu_{u}:\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})
as in
Definition

5.7
.

Lemma 5.9
.

The maps
\mu_{u}(0,-):\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})
and
\mu_{u}(0,-):\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})
are based homotopic to
\operatorname{id}:\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})
.

Proof.

Note that from the proof of
Theorem

2.18
, the following map is continuous:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Iso}^{ev}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})

\displaystyle(\iota,\varphi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(\iota))(\varphi)

Set
v:=u^{*}\in\operatorname{Iso}^{ev}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})
. As
\operatorname{Iso}^{ev}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})
is path connected in the strong-
*
topology (
Lemma

5.8
), there is a path

p:[0,1]\rightarrow\operatorname{Iso}^{ev}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})

such that
p(0)=u^{*}
and
p(1)=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}
.

Thus,
H:\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})
, given by
H(\varphi,t)=(1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}p_{t}^{*})(s(\varphi,0))
is a based homotopy between
\mu_{u}(-,0)
and
\operatorname{id}
. The case of
\mu_{u}(0,-)
is similar.

∎

We have now shown that:

Theorem 5.10
.

If
A
is a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-graded
C^{*}
-algebra and
\mathcal{H}
is a graded Hilbert space whose even and odd parts are countable infinite dimensional. Then, for any
u\in\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ev}}(\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H})
,
(\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}),\mu_{u},0)
is an H-space.

Lemma 5.11
.

(\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}),\mu_{u},0)
is a homotopy associative
H
-space, that is, the following diagram commutes upto homotopy:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\times\mu_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u}\times 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u}}

Proof.

We expand the above diagram into the following:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\times s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\times\operatorname{Ad}(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\times 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u)\times 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u\oplus 1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(1\oplus u)}

We have to show that the outer rectangle commutes upto homotopy. To that end, we first observe that all the reactangles except the bottom right one commutes strictly, as follows from
Remark

12
and
Proposition

5.2
. For the homotopy commutativity of the bottom right rectangle, it suffices to show that the unitaries
u\circ(1\oplus u),u\circ(u\oplus 1)
are homotopic in the strong topology. But this follows from
Lemma

5.8
.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

(\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}),\mu_{u},0)
is a homotopy commutative
H
-space, that is, the following diagram commutes upto homotopy:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}}

Proof.

We expand the above diagram into the following:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\times\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}\oplus\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{flip}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}((x,y)\mapsto(y,x))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Ad}(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}}

Our goal is to show that the outer diagram commutes upto homotopy. To that end, we note that the top rectangle strictly commutes by
Proposition

5.2
, and the bottom one commutes up to homotopy since the two unitaries
u\circ((x,y)\mapsto(y,x))
and
u
are homotopic in the strong topology.
∎

Summarizing, we have:

Corollary 5.13
.

(\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}),\mu_{u},0)
is a commutative H-monoid.

Remark 13
.

It is in fact also true that
(\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}),\mu_{u},0)
is a commutative
H
-group. But we don’t use this fact in this article.

5.3
Sums preserve H-space structures

As the title of the subsection suggests, we now see how the sums described in the first part of this section go with the H-space structures. We summarize everything in the below proposition. Throughout there, we assume that H-space structures
\mu_{u},\mu_{u^{\prime}},...
as described in
Definition

5.7
have been fixed on the spaces
\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H}),\mathbb{K}(B,\mathcal{H}^{\prime})...
.

Proposition 5.14
.

1.

Let
\varphi:A\rightarrow B
be a graded
*
-homomorphism, and
\iota:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
an even isometry. Then the induced map
\mathbb{K}(\varphi,\iota):\mathbb{K}(A,\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(B,\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
preserves the
H
-space structure, in the sense that the following diagram commutes upto homotopy:

\hbox to247.39pt{\vbox to74.3pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 123.69217pt\lower-35.73721pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
